I just constantly feel like I'm messing everything up for the people around me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or on edge?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I don't think I feel nervous or, you know, jittery. It's not really an on-edge feeling. It's just more of that constant guilt I was talking about, feeling like I messed up. But no, I wouldn't say I feel anxious.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really sad. I mean, I don't find myself crying or feeling down in that way. It's really just... like I was saying before, it's more about feeling like a failure or that I'm letting everyone down. But I wouldn't say I feel sad most of the time, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying the things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, lately I can just... walk right past my sketchbook and not even pick up a pencil. I've been skipping volleyball, too, and I pretty much just ignore the group texts from my volunteer group. I don't really feel much of anything about it, which is weird, because those used to be the things that actually made me feel like myself. Now I just... don't have any interest in them.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's been about nine months. This whole stretch has just... been with me for a while now. It was easier to push through at first, but it definitely got a lot harder to hide once school and my work started slipping.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. My weight has stayed pretty much the same over the last nine months. I mean, maybe a pound or two fluctuating here and there, but nothing significant at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the activities you're currently doing, like your apprenticeship or spending time with friends?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I still show up to my apprenticeship because I need the hours, but I'm just going through the motions with the pipefitting and everything. I don't get any satisfaction out of it right now. And with my friends... I don't know, it's kind of the same. If I do see them, I'm mostly just sitting there. I wouldn't say I'm actually enjoying myself.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I don't think I feel nervous or, you know, jittery. It's not really an on-edge feeling. It's just more of that constant guilt I was talking about, feeling like I messed up. But no, I wouldn't say I feel anxious.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really sad. I mean, I don't find myself crying or feeling down in that way. It's really just... like I was saying before, it's more about feeling like a failure or that I'm letting everyone down. But I wouldn't say I feel sad most of the time, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying the things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Now I just... don't have any interest in them.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's been about nine months. My weight has stayed pretty much the same over the last nine months. I wouldn't say I'm actually enjoying myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I don't have some plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But... when I'm driving home after class, or just lying awake in bed, I do get these thoughts. I mean, I still show up to my apprenticeship because I need the hours, but I'm just going through the motions with the pipefitting and everything. I don't get any satisfaction out of it right now. And with my friends... I don't know, it's kind of the same. If I do see them, I'm mostly just sitting there. I wouldn't say I'm actually enjoying myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I don't have some plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But... when I'm driving home after class, or just lying awake in bed, I do get these thoughts. I just think that it would be easier if I didn't wake up tomorrow.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I don't really feel sad or depressed most of the day. It's more just that I don't care about things right now, like I was saying. But I wouldn't say I've had a solid two weeks where I actually felt depressed all day, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely have. Lately, I can just walk right past my sketchbook without even wanting to open it. I've been skipping volleyball, and I just ignore the group texts from my volunteer group. I don't really feel much of anything about missing them, even though those used to be the main things that made me feel like myself. I just don't have the interest right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt really down or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I think you asked something similar just a minute ago, but no, I really haven't. Like I said, I don't get that sad or down feeling. It's more just... not caring about anything, rather than actually feeling depressed all day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life when you felt depressed for most of the day?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Like I mentioned, I really don't get that sad, depressed feeling. It's just the lack of interest I was talking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode in the past where you felt depressed for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, I really haven't. I know you've asked about this a few different ways now, but my answer is still the same. I've never had a time, whether it was two weeks or longer, where I actually felt depressed. It's just the not caring about stuff that I deal with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life that lasted for several months where you felt depressed or down nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no. I've never really felt depressed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that would normally bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's exactly it. Like, lately... I can just walk right past my sketchbook and not even care. Now I just don't get anything out of them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in the past when you felt both sad and lost interest in things for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, not both of those together. I mean, like I was saying, I definitely get the part where I lose interest in things. But I don't get the sad part along with it.
